inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: I catch myself lining up papers and pill bottles over and over on the kitchen table, tapping each stack a certain number of times, even when I know mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of several weeks when my mood had eased enough that I was eating and getting dressed more normally, but I still felt sure the dates and names in the records were being changed to signal something.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have stopped teaching my craft It’s odd, because I used to like a little snack, but now it feels sort of automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you eat those crackers or sweets?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilty exactly… more like I notice afterward and think, “Why did I do that?” But then I get distracted—like, I’ll remember I meant to put the kettle on, or that the crackers are the kind my sister used to buy. Not always sad, no… I mean, sometimes I suppose I’m not feeling great, but it doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m upset, so I’ll eat crackers.” It’s more that my eating is all turned around lately. Some days I just forget lunch entirely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers and sweets, even though they don’t even taste right. It’s strange. I used to be more regular about things—my mother always said you should sit down for meals, not pick at food, but I seem to be doing that a lot now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the weight exactly… I haven’t been keeping track like I should. It’s more that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers and sweets, and they don’t even taste right. It’s more the eating that’s changed… I’ll forget lunch completely some days, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. They don’t even taste right, which is odd. My mother used to say you ought to sit down properly for meals, but I seem to keep doing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed with my clothes, no… or I haven’t paid close enough attention. What I do notice is more with the eating—some days I’ll forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’m standing at the counter with crackers or sweets, just picking at them. They don’t even taste right. It’s funny, my mother used to be so particular about sitting down for meals, but I don’t seem to do that lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have. I used to look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. I’d get my bag ready and set it by the door, you know, with the book or the little sewing things in it… and now it’ll just sit there. I look at it and think I should go, but I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually leave. The quilting ladies are nice, too—one of them always brings those little lemon cookies—but even that doesn’t really pull me out lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… most mornings, really. I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, low feeling sitting on me before I’ve even gotten dressed. Like the day’s already lost its color. I know that sounds a bit dramatic… my sister used to say I always noticed the light in a room, whether the curtains were open and all that, but lately it’s just… dull from the start.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a significant amount… I haven’t been weighing myself much. What I do notice is more with the eating—some days I’ll forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’m standing at the counter with crackers or sweets, just picking at them. I used to look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. I look at it and think I should go, but I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually leave. The quilting ladies are nice, too—one of them always brings those little lemon cookies—but even that doesn’t really pull me out lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… most mornings, really. I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, low feeling sitting on me before I’ve even gotten dressed. Like the day’s already lost its color. What I have noticed is my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and it doesn’t even taste right. I don’t know that it’s to deal with feeling sad, exactly. What made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because my eating’s been all off lately. Some days I just forget lunch entirely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers and sweets, even though they don’t even taste right. It’s strange. I used to be more regular about things—my mother always said you should sit down for meals, not pick at food, but I seem to be doing that a lot now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the weight exactly… I haven’t been keeping track like I should. It’s more that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers and sweets, and they don’t even taste right. My mother would’ve fussed at me for that, eating standing up like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t weighed myself, so I can’t say for sure. It’s more the eating that’s changed… I’ll forget lunch completely some days, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. They don’t even taste right, which is odd. My mother used to say you ought to sit down properly for meals, but I seem to keep doing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed with my clothes, no… or I haven’t paid close enough attention. What I do notice is more with the eating—some days I’ll forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’m standing at the counter with crackers or sweets, just picking at them. They don’t even taste right. It’s funny, my mother used to be so particular about sitting down for meals, but I don’t seem to do that lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have. I used to look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. I’d get my bag ready and set it by the door, you know, with the book or the little sewing things in it… and now it’ll just sit there. I look at it and think I should go, but I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually leave. The quilting ladies are nice, too—one of them always brings those little lemon cookies—but even that doesn’t really pull me out lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… most mornings, really. I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, low feeling sitting on me before I’ve even gotten dressed. Like the day’s already lost its color. I know that sounds a bit dramatic… my sister used to say I always noticed the light in a room, whether the curtains were open and all that, but lately it’s just… dull from the start.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a significant amount… I haven’t been weighing myself much. What I have noticed, and it’s part of why I came in, is my eating is all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. And they don’t even taste right, which is strange… I used to enjoy little snacks with tea, especially those butter cookies my neighbor used to bring over. But now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit, such as feeling looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about my clothes exactly… I haven’t really been paying close attention to the waistband or anything. What I have noticed is my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and it doesn’t even taste right. It’s funny, I used to have a real liking for a little sweet with tea… but now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s noticeable on the scale, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track like that. But the eating is what made me come in… it’s all gotten turned around. Some days I just forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and they don’t even taste right. It’s odd, because I used to like a little snack, but now it feels sort of automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you eat those crackers or sweets?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilty exactly… more like I notice afterward and think, “Why did I do that?” But then I get distracted—like, I’ll remember I meant to put the kettle on, or that the crackers are the kind my sister used to buy. So… no, not really guilt, I don’t think. Just kind of odd.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of interest in things like your watercolor class and quilting circle because you just don't feel like doing them or because you feel too tired or overwhelmed to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s tired exactly. Sometimes I’m tired, sure, but mostly it’s like I used to look forward to watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and even book club. I’d have my bag ready by the door, with the little tin of pencils—well, not for quilting, but you know what I mean—and now the bag just sits there. I see it and think I should go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to actually leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Not always sad, no… I mean, sometimes I suppose I’m not feeling great, but it doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m upset, so I’ll eat crackers.” It’s more that my eating is all turned around lately. Some days I forget lunch entirely, and then other days I’m just there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and they don’t even taste right. It’s a funny thing, because I used to keep crackers for company, you know, in case someone stopped by… but now I’m the one standing there with the box open.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers or sweets helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it really helps, no. It’s not like I eat them and feel comforted. Mostly what made me come in is my eating’s gotten all turned around—some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and nothing even tastes right. I used to keep those little sleeves of crackers for guests, with the good napkins in the drawer… but now I’ll look down and I’ve opened them myself. So, no, not better exactly. Just… strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat crackers or sweets in an attempt to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really an attempt, no. It’s just gotten strange.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I used to look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. I’d get my bag ready and set it by the door, you know, with the book or the little sewing things in it… and now it’ll just sit there. I look at it and think I should go, but I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually leave. The quilting ladies are nice, too—one of them always brings those little lemon cookies—but even that doesn’t really pull me out lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… most mornings, really. I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, low feeling sitting on me before I’ve even gotten dressed. Like the day’s already lost its color. I know that sounds a bit dramatic… my sister used to say I always noticed the light in a room, whether the curtains were open and all that, but lately it’s just… dull from the start.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a significant amount… I haven’t been weighing myself much. What I have noticed, and it’s part of why I came in, is my eating is all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. And they don’t even taste right, which is strange… I used to enjoy little snacks with tea, especially those butter cookies my neighbor used to bring over. But now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit, such as feeling looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about my clothes exactly… I haven’t really been paying close attention to the waistband or anything. What I have noticed is my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and it doesn’t even taste right. It’s funny, I used to have a real liking for a little sweet with tea… but now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s noticeable on the scale, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track like that. But the eating is what made me come in… it’s all gotten turned around. It’s odd, because I used to like a little snack, but now it feels sort of automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you eat those crackers or sweets?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilty exactly… more like I notice afterward and think, “Why did I do that?” But then I get distracted—like, I’ll remember I meant to put the kettle on, or that the crackers are the kind my sister used to buy. Sometimes I’m tired, sure, but mostly it’s like I used to look forward to watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and even book club. I’d have my bag ready by the door, with the little tin of pencils—well, not for quilting, but you know what I mean—and now the bag just sits there. I see it and think I should go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to actually leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Not always sad, no… I mean, sometimes I suppose I’m not feeling great, but it doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m upset, so I’ll eat crackers.” It’s more that my eating is all turned around lately. It’s odd, because I used to like a little snack, but now it feels sort of automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you eat those crackers or sweets?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilty exactly… more like I notice afterward and think, “Why did I do that?” But then I get distracted—like, I’ll remember I meant to put the kettle on, or that the crackers are the kind my sister used to buy. So… no, not really guilt, I don’t think. Just kind of odd.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of interest in things like your watercolor class and quilting circle because you just don't feel like doing them or because you feel too tired or overwhelmed to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s tired exactly. Sometimes I’m tired, sure, but mostly it’s like I used to look forward to watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and even book club. I see it and think I should go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to actually leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Not always sad, no… I mean, sometimes I suppose I’m not feeling great, but it doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m upset, so I’ll eat crackers.” It’s more that my eating is all turned around lately. It’s a funny thing, because I used to keep crackers for company, you know, in case someone stopped by… but now I’m the one standing there with the box open.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers or sweets helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it really helps, no. I used to keep those little sleeves of crackers for guests, with the good napkins in the drawer… but now I’ll look down and I’ve opened them myself. So, no, not better exactly. Just… strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat crackers or sweets in an attempt to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really an attempt, no. It’s more that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. I’ll think, “Why am I even eating this?” but I keep picking at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating when you're not hungry is something you do to deal with feeling sad or down?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s to deal with feeling sad, exactly. I used to be more regular about things—my mother always said you should sit down for meals, not pick at food, but I seem to be doing that a lot now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the weight exactly… I haven’t been keeping track like I should. It’s more that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers and sweets, and they don’t even taste right. My mother would’ve fussed at me for that, eating standing up like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t weighed myself, so I can’t say for sure. It’s more the eating that’s changed… I’ll forget lunch completely some days, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. They don’t even taste right, which is odd. My mother used to say you ought to sit down properly for meals, but I seem to keep doing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed with my clothes, no… or I haven’t paid close enough attention. What I do notice is more with the eating—some days I’ll forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’m standing at the counter with crackers or sweets, just picking at them. They don’t even taste right. It’s funny, my mother used to be so particular about sitting down for meals, but I don’t seem to do that lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have. I used to look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. I’d get my bag ready and set it by the door, you know, with the book or the little sewing things in it… and now it’ll just sit there. I look at it and think I should go, but I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually leave. The quilting ladies are nice, too—one of them always brings those little lemon cookies—but even that doesn’t really pull me out lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… most mornings, really. I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, low feeling sitting on me before I’ve even gotten dressed. Like the day’s already lost its color. I know that sounds a bit dramatic… my sister used to say I always noticed the light in a room, whether the curtains were open and all that, but lately it’s just… dull from the start.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a significant amount… I haven’t been weighing myself much. What I have noticed, and it’s part of why I came in, is my eating is all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. And they don’t even taste right, which is strange… I used to enjoy little snacks with tea, especially those butter cookies my neighbor used to bring over. But now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit, such as feeling looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about my clothes exactly… I haven’t really been paying close attention to the waistband or anything. What I have noticed is my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and it doesn’t even taste right. It’s funny, I used to have a real liking for a little sweet with tea… but now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s noticeable on the scale, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track like that. But the eating is what made me come in… it’s all gotten turned around. Some days I just forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and they don’t even taste right. It’s odd, because I used to like a little snack, but now it feels sort of automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you eat those crackers or sweets?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilty exactly… more like I notice afterward and think, “Why did I do that?” But then I get distracted—like, I’ll remember I meant to put the kettle on, or that the crackers are the kind my sister used to buy. So… no, not really guilt, I don’t think. Just kind of odd.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of interest in things like your watercolor class and quilting circle because you just don't feel like doing them or because you feel too tired or overwhelmed to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s tired exactly. Sometimes I’m tired, sure, but mostly it’s like I used to look forward to watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and even book club. I’d have my bag ready by the door, with the little tin of pencils—well, not for quilting, but you know what I mean—and now the bag just sits there. I see it and think I should go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to actually leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Not always sad, no… I mean, sometimes I suppose I’m not feeling great, but it doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m upset, so I’ll eat crackers.” It’s more that my eating is all turned around lately. Some days I forget lunch entirely, and then other days I’m just there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and they don’t even taste right. It’s a funny thing, because I used to keep crackers for company, you know, in case someone stopped by… but now I’m the one standing there with the box open.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers or sweets helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it really helps, no. It’s not like I eat them and feel comforted. Mostly what made me come in is my eating’s gotten all turned around—some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and nothing even tastes right. I used to keep those little sleeves of crackers for guests, with the good napkins in the drawer… but now I’ll look down and I’ve opened them myself. So, no, not better exactly. Just… strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat crackers or sweets in an attempt to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really an attempt, no. It’s more that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. I’ll think, “Why am I even eating this?” but I keep picking at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating when you're not hungry is something you do to deal with feeling sad or down?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s to deal with feeling sad, exactly. What made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing there at the counter with crackers or sweets, not even hungry really, and nothing tastes right anyway. I used to be so particular about keeping things nice in the pantry, you know, the crackers in the tin… now I just find myself picking at them. So, no, I don’t think of it as helping the sadness. It’s just gotten strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is a way for you to cope with your feelings?\n\nPatient: Maybe, but I don’t think of it that neatly. It’s more like my eating’s just gone sideways. Some days I’ll get all the way to afternoon and realize I never had lunch, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or something sweet, and it doesn’t even taste right.\n\nI used to keep those crackers for when someone came by, you know, with tea… not that many people come by now. But anyway, I don’t know if I’d call it coping. It’s just… something’s off with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're not hungry as a way to cope with your feelings?\n\nPatient: I suppose it might look that way, but I don’t really think, “I’m upset, so I’ll eat.” It’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll forget lunch completely, and then another day I’m standing at the counter eating crackers and sweets when I’m not even hungry, and they don’t taste right anyway.\n\nI used to be more careful about meals, you know… but now it’s just odd. Yes, I think it was after I started feeling so sad… or around then, anyway. That’s really what made me come in, because my eating just got all turned around.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because my eating’s been all off lately. Some days I just forget lunch entirely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers and sweets, even though they don’t even taste right. It’s strange. I used to be more regular about things—my mother always said you should sit down for meals, not pick at food, but I seem to be doing that a lot now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the weight exactly… I haven’t been keeping track like I should. It’s more that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers and sweets, and they don’t even taste right. My mother would’ve fussed at me for that, eating standing up like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t weighed myself, so I can’t say for sure. It’s more the eating that’s changed… I’ll forget lunch completely some days, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. They don’t even taste right, which is odd. My mother used to say you ought to sit down properly for meals, but I seem to keep doing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed with my clothes, no… or I haven’t paid close enough attention. What I do notice is more with the eating—some days I’ll forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’m standing at the counter with crackers or sweets, just picking at them. They don’t even taste right. It’s funny, my mother used to be so particular about sitting down for meals, but I don’t seem to do that lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have. I used to look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. I’d get my bag ready and set it by the door, you know, with the book or the little sewing things in it… and now it’ll just sit there. I look at it and think I should go, but I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually leave. The quilting ladies are nice, too—one of them always brings those little lemon cookies—but even that doesn’t really pull me out lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… most mornings, really. I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, low feeling sitting on me before I’ve even gotten dressed. Like the day’s already lost its color. I know that sounds a bit dramatic… my sister used to say I always noticed the light in a room, whether the curtains were open and all that, but lately it’s just… dull from the start.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a significant amount… I haven’t been weighing myself much. What I have noticed, and it’s part of why I came in, is my eating is all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets. And they don’t even taste right, which is strange… I used to enjoy little snacks with tea, especially those butter cookies my neighbor used to bring over. But now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit, such as feeling looser than they used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about my clothes exactly… I haven’t really been paying close attention to the waistband or anything. What I have noticed is my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and it doesn’t even taste right. It’s funny, I used to have a real liking for a little sweet with tea… but now it’s more like I’m just doing it without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s noticeable on the scale, honestly. I haven’t been keeping track like that. But the eating is what made me come in… it’s all gotten turned around. Some days I just forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and they don’t even taste right. It’s odd, because I used to like a little snack, but now it feels sort of automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you eat those crackers or sweets?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilty exactly… more like I notice afterward and think, “Why did I do that?” But then I get distracted—like, I’ll remember I meant to put the kettle on, or that the crackers are the kind my sister used to buy. So… no, not really guilt, I don’t think. Just kind of odd.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of interest in things like your watercolor class and quilting circle because you just don't feel like doing them or because you feel too tired or overwhelmed to participate?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s tired exactly. Sometimes I’m tired, sure, but mostly it’s like I used to look forward to watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and even book club. I’d have my bag ready by the door, with the little tin of pencils—well, not for quilting, but you know what I mean—and now the bag just sits there. I see it and think I should go, but I just can’t make myself care enough to actually leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling sad or upset?\n\nPatient: Not always sad, no… I mean, sometimes I suppose I’m not feeling great, but it doesn’t feel like, “Oh, I’m upset, so I’ll eat crackers.” It’s more that my eating is all turned around lately. Some days I forget lunch entirely, and then other days I’m just there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and they don’t even taste right. It’s a funny thing, because I used to keep crackers for company, you know, in case someone stopped by… but now I’m the one standing there with the box open.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers or sweets helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it really helps, no. It’s not like I eat them and feel comforted. Mostly what made me come in is my eating’s gotten all turned around—some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, and nothing even tastes right. I used to keep those little sleeves of crackers for guests, with the good napkins in the drawer… but now I’ll look down and I’ve opened them myself. So, no, not better exactly. Just… strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat crackers or sweets in an attempt to improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s really an attempt, no. It’s more that my eating’s gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right. I’ll think, “Why am I even eating this?” but I keep picking at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating when you're not hungry is something you do to deal with feeling sad or down?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s to deal with feeling sad, exactly. What made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing there at the counter with crackers or sweets, not even hungry really, and nothing tastes right anyway. I used to be so particular about keeping things nice in the pantry, you know, the crackers in the tin… now I just find myself picking at them. So, no, I don’t think of it as helping the sadness. It’s just gotten strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is a way for you to cope with your feelings?\n\nPatient: Maybe, but I don’t think of it that neatly. It’s more like my eating’s just gone sideways. Some days I’ll get all the way to afternoon and realize I never had lunch, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or something sweet, and it doesn’t even taste right.\n\nI used to keep those crackers for when someone came by, you know, with tea… not that many people come by now. But anyway, I don’t know if I’d call it coping. It’s just… something’s off with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're not hungry as a way to cope with your feelings?\n\nPatient: I suppose it might look that way, but I don’t really think, “I’m upset, so I’ll eat.” It’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around. It’s just… something’s off with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're not hungry as a way to cope with your feelings?\n\nPatient: I suppose it might look that way, but I don’t really think, “I’m upset, so I’ll eat.” It’s more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll forget lunch completely, and then another day I’m standing at the counter eating crackers and sweets when I’m not even hungry, and they don’t taste right anyway.\n\nI used to be more careful about meals, you know… but now it’s just odd. So I don’t know if I’d call it coping. It’s just not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you deal with your sad feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it helps, no. It’s more like… my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m just standing at the counter eating crackers or something sweet, even though I’m not hungry.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, it doesn’t even taste right. I used to keep crackers for company, in a little tin—well, I don’t know why I’m telling you that. But no, I wouldn’t say it helps with being sad. It’s just… not like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating is a way for you to try to feel better when you're sad?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it really makes me feel better, no. It’s more that my eating is all mixed up lately. Some days I’ll get to the afternoon and realize I forgot lunch, and then other times I’m at the counter eating crackers or something sweet when I’m not even hungry.\n\nAnd it doesn’t taste right, either, which is strange because I used to like those little sweet biscuits with tea. I used to put them out if someone stopped by… but that’s not really what you asked. I just mean, I don’t know if I’m trying to feel better. It’s just gotten turned around.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits changed after you started feeling sad?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it was after I started feeling so sad… or around then, anyway. That’s really what made me come in, because my eating just got all turned around.\n\nSome days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers and sweets when I’m not even hungry. And nothing tastes right, which is odd… I used to enjoy little things like that. But yes, it changed."
